Postea prohibet additiones, hoc est, omnes corruptelas, omnia idola, omnes impios cultus, omnes asseverationes de Deo, & de nostris moribus, quae non congruunt ad normam verbi Dei.

Secunda sententia loquitur de mediocritate vitae. Ostendit duo praecipua bona esse, veram agnitionem Dei, & mediocrem gradum in communibus laboribus seu officiis vitae, Quia vult Deus hominem non esse ociosum, & inutile.

onus terrae, sed suo loco in sua vocatione iuvare communem societatem, ut scriptum est, In sudore vultus tui comedas panem tuum. Item, Labores manuum tuarum manducabis. Item ad Thessal. Propria facite &c.

Optat autem mediocrem gradum, Quia plaerunque homines in secundis rebus fiunt negligentiores. Sicut scriptum est, Sedit populus manducare & bibere, & surrexit ludere &c. Negligentes minus anxie invocant Deum, quaerunt voluptates, fiunt superbi, curiosi, non necessaria moventes. Sicut David & Salomon in ocio facti sunt negligentiores. Et Iosias elatus prioribus successibus, movit bellum non necessarium.

Denique quotidiana exempla ostendunt utrumque periculosum esse, nimiam opulentiam & extremam inopiam, quia inopes, cum non didicerint honestos labores, confugiunt ad assentationes & ad malas artes. Quia vero petit a Deo veritatem, & mediocritatem facultatum, testatur, utrumque beneficium Dei esse, & petendum esse suo ordine, & ordinatos a Deo labores suscipiendos. Sicut hic nominat Panem demensum.

## 30:11
This kind makes a reproach to the father, and does not bless the mother.

The following sentences are for the most part narratives, others are about other matters. This next one describes notable vices spreading everywhere, such as these complaints, Nowhere is faith safe. Or, ὀυκ ὁσίη γενεὴ κακὰ ῥάπτειν ἀλλὴλοισιν.

Thus he says here, There is a generation that curses its parents, That is, there is a certain kind of stubborn person, impatient of all discipline. Now let everyone consider how widely this evil spreads, & how destructive it is to the human race.

Another plague is hypocrisy. There is a kind that seems righteous to itself, about which it is said in the Gospel, when they have a beam in their own eyes, they criticize the mote in the eyes of others, as now there are many censors of others, & they judge themselves to be without any stain, when nevertheless they are not without errors & vices.

The third kind is the ambitious, greedy for power & glory.

The fourth kind is slanderers, to whom he attributes cruelty, Just as it is said elsewhere, Their feet are swift to shed blood.

Let us know that these kinds are recited so that, being reminded, we may recognize the evils of the world, & pray to God that He may rule & defend us. Then also let there be in us this diligence of avoiding such vices, of seeking the true assembly of the Church, & of living modestly in it, & of rightly celebrating God &c.

By this simple explanation it is sufficiently indicated that the summary is such that the text may be simply understood.

Latin:

Hoc genus patri convicium facit, nec benedicit matri.

Sequentes sententiae pleraeque narrationes sunt, aliae de aliis materiis. Haec proxima describit vicia insignia passim grassantia, quales sunt hae querelae, Nusquam tuta fides. Vel, ὀυκ ὁσίη γενεὴ κακὰ ῥάπτειν ἀλλὴλοισιν.

Ita hic inquit, Est generatio, quae maledicit parentibus, Id est, est genus quoddam contumax, impatiens universae disciplinae. Iam cogitet quisque, quam late hoc malum grassetur, & quam pernicio sum sit generi humano.

Alia pestis est hypocrisis. Est genus, quod sibi videtur iustum, de quo in Evangelio dicitur, cum habeant trabem in oculis suis, reprehendunt festucam in aliorum oculis, ut nunc multi sunt censores aliorum, & se sine ulla labe esse iudicant, cum tamen non sint sine erroribus & viciis.

Tertium genus est, ambitiosi, cupidi potentiae & gloriae.

Quartum genus est, calumniatores, quibus tribuit crudelitatem, Sicut & alibi dicitur, Veloces pedes eorum ad effundendum sanguinem.

Haec genera ita recitari sciamus, ut commonefacti agnoscamus mala mundi, & oremus Deum, ut nos regat ac defendat. Deinde & in nobis haec diligentia sit vitandi talia vicia, quaerendi verum Ecclesiae coetum, & in eo modeste vivendi, & Deum recte celebrandi &c.

Hac simplici enarratione satis est indicatam esse summam, ut textus simpliciter intelligatur.

## 30:21
Three things disturb the earth.

Such also are the following complaints, The earth cannot sustain a servant who has attained power. Item, A foolish rich and powerful man &c. The former sentence is contained in this verse,

Nothing is harsher than a wretched man when he rises to the heights.

There are four small things.

There is also a charming comparison with small creatures, which he says excel the wise. However, he signifies that the great misery of human nature must be acknowledged, which fights with itself, knows the law and does not obey, and knowingly rushes against its own nature, and brings upon itself huge calamities, and not even in outstanding natures is virtue durable, as in Hercules, Themistocles, Alexander. But beasts obey their own nature, nor do they disagree with themselves.

He advises, therefore, that the causes of human infirmity and miseries must be sought, and that the remedies proposed in the Son of God must be sought.

If you have acted full, lifted up with pride, put your hand to your mouth.

He gives instruction concerning repentance and the amendment of errors. And he signifies that the cause of many falls is the trust in one's own wisdom and power, by which man departs from the divine voice, just as in all ages some have invented cults and dogmas, such as heretics, Pontiffs, and Monks. And the greatest part of the human race has errors, for this reason, because it admires its own opinions, nor does it suffer admonition. Just as another opinion is not easily admitted when the mind has been previously occupied by some persuasion, as Simonides says, δόξα βιάζεται την ἀλήθειαν, Opinion brings force to truth.

Latin:

Tria perturbant terram.

Tales sunt & sequentes quaerelae, Terra non potest sustinere servum potitum imperio. Item, Stultum divitem & potentem &c. Prior sententia continetur hoc versu,

Asperius nihil est misero cum surgit in altum.

Quatuor sunt minuta.

Est & collatio venusta cum minutis bestiolis, quas dicit antecellere sapientibus. Significat autem agnoscendam esse magnam miseriam naturae humanae, quae ipsa secum pugnat, novit legem & non obtemperat, & sciens ruit contra naturam suam, & accersit sibi ingentes calamitates, & ne quidem in praestantibus naturis virtus durabilis est, sicut in Hercule, Themistocle, Alexandro. At bestiae obtemperant suae naturae, nec a seipsis dissentiunt.

Monet igitur causas humanae infirmitatis & miseriarum quaerendas esse, & quaerenda remedia proposita in Filio Dei.

Si satur egisti elatus superbia,Manum opponito ori tuo.

Praecipit de paenitentia & emendatione errorum. Et causam significat multorum lapsuum esse fiduciam propriae sapientiae & potentiae, qua homo discedit a voce divina, sicut omnibus aetatibus finxerunt aliqui cultus & dogmata, sicut haeretici, Pontifices & Monachi. Et maxima pars generis humani habet errata, propter hanc causam, quia suas opiniones admiratur, nec admonitionem patitur. Sicut non facile admittitur alia sententia, quando mens persuasione aliqua antea occupata est, Ut Simonides inquit, δόξα βιάζεται την ἀλήθειαν, Opinio vim affert veritati.

## 30:33
He who presses milk, expresses butter. And he who wrings the nose too much, elicits blood. And he who sends forth anger, sends forth discords.

And he gives instruction concerning the moderation of discipline, and concerning the moderation of anger. Moderate governments are salutary. Just as it is also said in the saying of Plato, Both are best, moderate liberty, and tempered servitude. And both are worst, immoderate liberty, and immoderate servitude.

Latin:

Qui premit lac, exprimit butyrum. Et nimium emungens narem, elicit sanguinem. Et qui emittit iram, emittit discor-dias.

ET de moderatione disciplinae, & de moderatione irae praecipit. Imperia mediocria sunt salutaria. Sicut & in dicto Platonis dicitur, Utraque optima, libertas mediocris, & servitus moderata. Et utraque pessima, libertas immoderata, & servitus immodica.

## 31:1 Argument
CHAPTER XXXI.

The addition of the last Chapter contains three sentiments, the Prohibition of drunkenness, the Command concerning confession against those who oppress the innocent, as now many are killed on account of true doctrine.

The third part is a poem concerning the virtues of an honest mother of a family.

He attributes to her, however, the fear of God, by which appellation he understands the whole worship pleasing to God, as the Prophets often speak in this way.

The worship, however, as is known, embraces all necessary virtues, Odem, fear, trust in mercy, love, hope, the justice of a good conscience.

Specifically, however, in this poem some virtues are recited properly befitting a mother of a family, namely conjugal chastity, and love toward a husband without moroseness, assiduity in all economic labors. Parsimony and gentleness in the governance of the family, diligence in educating offspring, assiduity in staying at home avoiding wanderings, beneficence toward the poor, etc.

The definitions, however, of these virtues and their properties are known from the doctrine of the Decalogue, and let this poem be referred to the Decalogue, and let it be understood simply without allegory, and let it be considered as a mirror of a pious matron, let not foreign interpretations be attached.

It agrees, however, with the saying of Paul 1. Timoth. 2. The woman shall be saved through the generation of children, if she shall have remained in faith, love, sanctification, and temperance, where also the chief virtues and the duties of the vocation are recited, Faith and the whole labor of birth and education. Then the love of God and neighbor, which contains many virtues, justice toward all, gentleness, beneficence. Sanctification signifies chastity. Temperance signifies the measure in food and drink, Because gluttonies hinder invocation, and the duties of many other virtues, and irritate lusts, as it is said, And Venus in wines, Fire rages in fire.

Paul therefore delivers a most grave sentence, and a most sweet consolation, to be opposed to the hypocrisy of the Monks, and let the cavil be removed, when he says, The woman is saved, as if he were saying this, that these works are the price of eternal life. But as he says elsewhere, That it is necessary in all things to have a good conscience with faith. So here he affirms that in the woman there must be more virtues, just as he says concerning his own calling, Woe to me, if I do not preach the gospel. And yet the sentence remains firm, that on account of the Mediator she is received by faith, and the person is pleasing, not on account of her own dignity.

This therefore he says, The woman is saved having faith, and the other necessary virtues, which is to be declared. Just as these sayings are often declared elsewhere.

THE END.

Omnipotent, eternal and living God, eternal Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, who have revealed yourself to us your creatures with immense goodness, and have abundantly declared your secret will toward us, and your wonderful and ineffable counsel, concerning the liberation of the human race, and the restoration of the nature of the same, depraved by the fall of Adam, concluded before the foundation of the world with you in an admirable way, through the voice sounding in Paradise, and the propagation of your holy Word, and the gift of your Son, sent from your bosom. And who from heaven cried out concerning your Son our Lord Jesus Christ with a clear and salutary voice, This is my beloved Son, in whom I am well pleased, Hear him.

Creator, Redeemer, and Helper of all things, with your Son our Lord Jesus Christ, who reigns with you with equal power, and who revealed himself in Jerusalem, by teaching the people, and by performing many great and wonderful miracles, And your Holy Spirit, poured out upon the Apostles. Wise, good, merciful, Judge and strong, who said, I live, I do not desire the death of the sinner, but that he be converted and live. And who spoke through the mouth of David your servant, Call upon me in the day of your tribulation, and I will deliver you, and you shall glorify me, have mercy on me, and on my members, forgive me and them all our sins, whether by negligence, or ignorance, or omission, or commission, or πολυπραγμοσύνην, as human nature is weak and too unstable, committed, on account of your Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, whom you willed to be a victim for us all, λύτρον μεσιτὴν, ἱκέτην καὶ ἀρχὶερεα, by a wonderful and ineffable counsel, that you might declare your true and immense wrath against the sins of the human race, and your immense mercy, And sanctify, rule and restore the Schools, which are the treasury of your Church, And gather the Ministers of your Church, and the remaining members, which like winds are scattered and exiled in the circle of the lands, and lie miserably prostrate before your feet, And the works, which through them, admirable and salutary in the polities, and in your Church now failing and growing old, prepared by you, you have begun and helped, increase, and in them perfect, And take away all scandals in your Church, Repress the Mahometan impiety, and all the errors of all other Sects, help and kindle our hearts and minds with your Holy Spirit, in the acknowledgment and invocation of you, Kindle also in the members of your Church the light of the Gospel and the light of your word, And rule their hearts with your Holy Spirit, lest they rush into Epicurean furies or fanatical errors, And give them a firm, upright spirit, not fleeing the struggles and dangers of confession, not doubting, not Epicurean, nor a breast σκωπτικὸν, such as men playing have, fluctuating in opinions and by every wind of doctrine. Rule and confirm by your Holy Spirit the minds of the most illustrious Princes of the whole glorious house of Saxony, which was, is, and by your will and help, shall be the nurse of your Church, And incline the hearts of the highest governors to a salutary government of the polities, providing a nest and hospitality for the Bride of your Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, And restore a pious peace, both to all the estates of the Empire, and salutary to men of all orders, And console all the afflicted and captives by your word and Holy Spirit, and bend their minds to the true invocation of you, and to the good fruits of repentance, and nourish and defend them. And since you do all things salutary in your Church, be effective in the members of your Church, now in exile, Make them salutary organs and useful vessels of mercy for the polities, not instruments of wrath and damnation, not pernicious and destructive to your Church, But make them vessels of mercy and grace, And give to us all who ask the Holy Spirit, who may restrain and bridle in us depraved desires, and rule our minds and all our actions, so that in the true acknowledgment and invocation of you, to you, God the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, from a true and whole heart, according to the promise given to our first parents, issued in Paradise, and promulgated through the Prophets, and fulfilled 1514 years ago in Jerusalem, we may serve and do salutary things, And sanctify us all to eternal life, which we, with all creatures, who groan and travail in pain together with us, devoutly, ardently, and daily hope for and expect.

PRAYER OF THE EXILED CHURCH TO GOD THE FATHER.

AMEN.

Latin:

CAPUT XXXI.

Postremi Capitis additio continet tres sententias, Prohibitionem ebrietatis, Mandatum de confessione adversus eos, qui opprimunt innocentes, Ut nunc multi propter veram doctrinam interficiuntur.

Tertia pars est carmen de virtutibus honestae matrisfamilas.

Tribuit autem ei timorem Dei, qua appellatione intelligit totum cultum Deo placentem, ut saepe Prophetae hoc modo loquuntur.

Complectitur autem cultus, ut notum est, omnes virtutes necessarias, Odem, timorem, fiduciam misericordiae, dilectionem, spem, iusticiam bonae conscientiae.

Nominatim vero in hoc carmine virtutes aliquae recitantur proprie connenientes matrifamilias, videlicet castitas coniugalis, & amor erga maritum sine morositate, sedulitas in omnibus laboribus oeconomicis. Parsimonia & mansuetudo in gubernatione familiae, diligentia in educanda sobole, assiduitas domesticavitans expatiationes, beneficentia erga pauperes &c.

Definitiones autem harum virtutum & proprietates notae sunt ex doctrim Decalogi, & ad Decalogum referatur hoc carmen, & simpliciter sine allegoria intelligatur, & tanquam speculum piae matronae consideretur, non affingantur peregrinae interpretationes.

Convenit autem cum dicto Pauli 1. Timoth. 2. Salvabitur mulier per filiorum generationem, si manserit in fide, dilectione, sanctificatione, & temperantis, Ubi etiam virtutes praecipuae & officia vocationis recitantur, Fides & totus lbor partus & educationis. Deinde dilectio Dei & proximi, quae continet moltas virtutes, iusticiam erga omnes, mansuetudinem, beneficentiam. Sanctificatio significat castitatem. Temperantia modum in cibo & potu, Quia helluationes impediunt invocationem, & multarum aliarum virtutum officia, & irritant libidines, ut dicitur, Et Venus in vinis, Ignis in igne furit.

Tradit igitur Paulus gravissimam sententiam, & dulcissimam consolationem, opponendam hypocrisi Monachorum, & removeatur cavillatio, cum dicit, Sic salvatur mulier, quasi hoc dicat, haec opera esse precium vitae aeternae. Sed sicut alibi inquit, Oportere in omnibus bonam conscientiam esse cum fide. Ita hic affirmat in muliere plures virtutes esse oportere, sicut de sua vocatione dicit, Vae mihi, si non evangelizo. Et tamen manet firma sententia, quod propter Mediatorem fide recipiatur, & placeat persona, non propter propriam dignitatem.

Hoc igitur dicit, Salvatur mulier habens fidem, & caeteras virtutes necessarias, quod declarandum est. Sicut haec dicta alias saepe declarantur.

FINIS.

Omnipotens, aeterne & vive Deus, aeterne Pater Domini nostri Iesu Christi, qui te immensa bonitate nobis creaturis tuis patefecisti, & tuam arcanam voluntatem erga nos, tuumque mirandum & inenarrabile consilium, de liberatione generis humani, & restauratione naturae eiusdem, per lapsum Adae depravatae, ante mundi constitionem apud te admirando modo conclusum, per vocem sonantem in Paradiso, & propagationem Verbi tui sancti, & donationem Filii tui, ex sinu tuo missi, copiose declarasti. Et qui de Filio tuo Domino nostro Iesu Christo de caelo clara & salutari voce clamasti, Hic est Filius meus dilectus, in quo ego delector, Hunc audite.

Creator, Redemptor, & Opitulator omnium rerum, cum Filio tuo Domino nostro Iesu Caeristo, qui tecum aequali potentia regnat, & qui se patefecit in Ierusalem, docendo populum, & edendis multis magnis & mirandis miraculis, Et Spiritu sancto tuo, effuso in Apostolos. Sapiens, bone, misericors, Iudex & fortis, qui dixisti, Vivo ego, nolo mortem peccatoris, sed ut convertatur & vivat. Et qui per os David servi tui locutus es, Invoca me in die tribulationis tuae, & ego eripiam te, & glorificabis me, miserere mei, & membrorum meorum, remitte mihi & illis omnia peccata nostra, vel per negligentiam, vel ignorantiam, vel omissionem, vel commissionem, aut πολυπραγμοσύνην, ut est natura humana imbecillis & labilis nimium, commissa, propter Filium tuum, Dominum nostrum Iesum Christum, quem voluisti pro nobis omnibus esse victimam, λύτρον μεσιτὴν, ἱκέτην καὶ ἀρχὶερεα, mirabili & inenarrabili consilio, ut iram tuam veram & ingentem, adversus peccata generis humani, & immensam misericordiam declarares, Et sanctifica, rege & restitue Scholas, quae sunt thesaurus Ecclesiae tuae, Et collige Ecclesiae tuae Ministros, & reliqua membra, quae quasi venti in orbe terrarum sparsim dissipata exulant, & prostrata coram pedibus tuis misere iacent, Et opera, quae per ea admiranda & salutaria in politiis, & Ecclesia tua iam deficiente & senescente, a te praeparata, inchoasti & adiuvisti, adauge, & in eis perfice, Et tolle omnia scandala in Ecclesia tua, Reprime impietatem Mahometicam, & omnes omnium aliarum Sectarum errores, iuva & accende corda & animos nostros Spiritu sancto tuo, in agnitione & invocatione tui, Accende etiam in membris Ecclesiae tuae lumen Evangelii & lucem verbi tui, Et rege corda eorum Spiritu sancto tuo, ne in furores Epicureos aut fanaticos errores ruant, Et da illis spiritum firmum, rectum, non fugientem certamina & pericula consessionis, non dubitantem, non Epicureum, nec pectus σκωπτικὸν, quale habent homines ludentes, opinionibus & quonis vento doctrinae fluctuantes. Rege & confirma Spiritu sancto tuo mentes illustrissimorum Principum domus totius Saxoniae gloriosae, quae fuit, est, & te volente & adiuvante, erit nutrix Ecclesiae tuae, Et inclina corda summorum gubernatorum ad salutarem politiis gubernationem, praebentibus nidum & hospicium Sponsae, Filii tui, Domini nostri Iesu Christi, Et restitue pacem piam, & omnibus Imperii statibus, & cunctorum ordinum hominibus salutarem, Et consolare afflictos & captivos omnes verbo & Spiritu sancto tuo, & flecte illorum mentes ad veram invocationem tui, & ad bonos fructus paenitentiae, & ale & defende eos. Et cum omnia facias in Ecclesia tua salutaria, sis in membris Ecclesiae tuae, iam exulantis, efficax, Fac otgana ex eis salutaria & utilia politiis vasa misericordiae, non irae & damnationis instrumenta, non perniciosa & exitialia Ecclesiae tuae, Sed fac eos vasa misericordiae & gratiae, Et da nobis omnibus petentibus Spiritum sanctum, qui compescae & frenet in nobis cupiditates pravas, & regat mentes & omnes actiones nostras, ut in vera agnitione & invocatione tui, tibi Deo Patri Domini nostri Iesu Christi, ex vero & toto corde, iuxta datam primis parentibus nostris promissionem, editam in Paradiso, & promulgatam per Prophetas, & ante annos 1514. in Ierusalem impletam, serviamus & salutaria faciamus, Et sanctifica nos omnes ad vitam aeternam, quam nos, cum creaturis omnibus, quae simul nobiscum congemiscunt, & parturiunt, devotis precibus ardenter & quotidie speramus & expectamus.

ORATIO ECCLESIAE EXULANTIS AD DEUM PATREM.

AMEN.
